     8→
     9→export type ApiClientConfig = {
    10→  /** Base URL for the CipherBox API (e.g., "http://localhost:3000") */
    11→  baseUrl: string;
    12→  /** Returns a valid access token. Consumer owns refresh logic. */
    13→  getAccessToken: () => Promise<string>;
    14→  /** Called on 401 response to obtain a new access token. If provided, the failed request is retried once with the new token. */
    15→  refreshAccessToken?: () => Promise<string>;
    16→  /** Send cookies cross-origin (needed for HTTP-only refresh token cookie in web). */
    17→  withCredentials?: boolean;
    18→  /** Called when token refresh fails (e.g., clear stores, redirect to login). */
    19→  onRefreshFailure?: () => void;
    20→  /** Extra headers sent with every request (e.g., throttle bypass for testing). */
    21→  defaultHeaders?: Record<string, string>;
    22→};
    23→
    24→/**
    25→ * Create a configured axios instance for CipherBox API calls.
    26→ * No Zustand, no import.meta.env -- all config injected by consumer.
    27→ */
    28→export function createAxiosInstance(config: ApiClientConfig): AxiosInstance {
    29→  const instance = axios.create({
    30→    baseURL: config.baseUrl,
    31→    withCredentials: config.withCredentials ?? false,
    32→    headers: config.defaultHeaders,
    33→  });
    34→  instance.interceptors.request.use(async (reqConfig) => {
    35→    const token = await config.getAccessToken();
    36→    if (token) {
    37→      reqConfig.headers.set('Authorization', `Bearer ${token}`);
    38→    }
    39→    return reqConfig;
    40→  });
    41→  return instance;
    42→}
    43→
    44→/**
    45→ * Custom instance function for orval-generated code.
    46→ * Matches the signature orval expects from a mutator.
    47→ * The consumer must call setApiClientConfig() before using generated functions.
    48→ */
    49→let _config: ApiClientConfig | null = null;
    50→let _instance: AxiosInstance | null = null;
    51→
    52→export function setApiClientConfig(config: ApiClientConfig): void {
    53→  _config = config;
    54→  _instance = null; // Reset cached instance when config changes
    55→}
    56→
    57→export function getApiClientConfig(): ApiClientConfig {
    58→  if (!_config) throw new Error('API client not configured. Call setApiClientConfig() first.');
    59→  return _config;
    60→}
    61→
    62→// Shared refresh promise eliminates race condition where multiple concurrent
    63→// 401 responses each trigger their own refresh before the first completes.
    64→let _refreshPromise: Promise<string> | null = null;
    65→
    66→function getCachedInstance(): AxiosInstance {
    67→  if (!_instance) {
    68→    const clientConfig = getApiClientConfig();
    69→    _instance = axios.create({
    70→      baseURL: clientConfig.baseUrl,
    71→      withCredentials: clientConfig.withCredentials ?? false,
    72→      headers: clientConfig.defaultHeaders,
    73→    });
    74→
    75→    // 401 response interceptor: refresh token and retry once
    76→    if (clientConfig.refreshAccessToken) {
    77→      const refreshFn = clientConfig.refreshAccessToken;
    78→      const onFailure = clientConfig.onRefreshFailure;
    79→
    80→      _instance.interceptors.response.use(
    81→        (response) => response,
    82→        async (error: AxiosError) => {
    83→          const originalRequest = error.config as
    84→            | (AxiosRequestConfig & { _retry?: boolean })
    85→            | undefined;
    86→          if (!originalRequest) throw error;
    87→
    88→          // Don't retry refresh endpoint to avoid infinite loop
    89→          const isRefreshRequest = originalRequest.url?.includes('/auth/refresh');
    90→
    91→          if (error.response?.status === 401 && !originalRequest._retry && !isRefreshRequest) {
    92→            originalRequest._retry = true;
    93→
    94→            if (!_refreshPromise) {
    95→              // First 401 handler: create the refresh promise synchronously
    96→              _refreshPromise = refreshFn()
    97→                .catch((refreshError) => {
    98→                  onFailure?.();
    99→                  throw refreshError;
   100→                })
   101→                .finally(() => {
   102→                  _refreshPromise = null;
   103→                });
   104→            }
   105→
   106→            // All concurrent 401 handlers await the same promise.
   107→            const token = await _refreshPromise;
   108→            originalRequest.headers = originalRequest.headers ?? {};
   109→            originalRequest.headers['Authorization'] = `Bearer ${token}`;
   110→            return getCachedInstance().request(originalRequest);
   111→          }
   112→
   113→          throw error;
   114→        }
   115→      );
   116→    }
   117→  }
   118→  return _instance;
   119→}
   120→
   121→export const customInstance = async <T>(
   122→  config: AxiosRequestConfig,
   123→  options?: AxiosRequestConfig & { _axiosInstance?: AxiosInstance }
   124→): Promise<T> => {
   125→  const { _axiosInstance, ...restOptions } = options ?? {};
   126→
   127→  if (_axiosInstance) {
   128→    // Instance-scoped path: the provided instance has its own interceptors for auth
   129→    const response = await _axiosInstance.request<T>({
   130→      ...config,
   131→      ...restOptions,
   132→      headers: {
   133→        ...config.headers,
   134→        ...restOptions.headers,
   135→      },
   136→    });
   137→    return response.data;
   138→  }
   139→
   140→  // Singleton path: existing behavior
   141→  const clientConfig = getApiClientConfig();
   142→  const token = await clientConfig.getAccessToken();
   143→  const instance = getCachedInstance();
   144→
   145→  const response = await instance.request<T>({
   146→    ...config,
   147→    ...restOptions,
   148→    headers: {
   149→      ...config.headers,
   150→      ...restOptions.headers,
   151→      ...(token ? { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } : {}),
   152→    },
   153→  });
   154→
   155→  return response.data;
   156→};
   157→
   158→export default customInstance;
   159→
   160→export type ErrorType<Error> = AxiosError<Error>;
   161→export type BodyType<BodyData> = BodyData;
   162→
   163→// Re-export axios types that consumers need for IPFS upload (progress, cancel)
   164→export type { AxiosInstance, AxiosProgressEvent, CancelToken };
   165→export { default as axios } from 'axios';
   166→
```

> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. The cleanest solution:

1. Enhance `createAxiosInstance` with 401 refresh support
2. Let `setApiClientConfig` accept a pre-built instance
3. `api-config.ts` creates the full instance, passes it to `setApiClientConfig`, and exports it
4. `getCachedInstance()` returns the pre-built instance (no duplicate creation)
5. CipherBoxClient accepts an external instance
6. `sdk-provider.ts` passes the shared instance

This gives ONE instance used by everything — generated function calls via singleton AND CipherBoxClient operations.

  "content": "import axios, {\n  type AxiosInstance,\n  type AxiosRequestConfig,\n  type AxiosError,\n  type AxiosProgressEvent,\n  type CancelToken,\n} from 'axios';\n\nexport type ApiClientConfig = {\n  /** Base URL for the CipherBox API (e.g., \"http://localhost:3000\") */\n  baseUrl: string;\n  /** Returns a valid access token. Consumer owns refresh logic. */\n  getAccessToken: () => Promise<string>;\n  /** Called on 401 response to obtain a new access token. If provided, the failed request is retried once with the new token. */\n  refreshAccessToken?: () => Promise<string>;\n  /** Send cookies cross-origin (needed for HTTP-only refresh token cookie in web). */\n  withCredentials?: boolean;\n  /** Called when token refresh fails (e.g., clear stores, redirect to login). */\n  onRefreshFailure?: () => void;\n  /** Extra headers sent with every request (e.g., throttle bypass for testing). */\n  defaultHeaders?: Record<string, string>;\n};\n\n/**\n * Create a configured axios instance for CipherBox API calls.\n * No Zustand, no import.meta.env -- all config injected by consumer.\n *\n * Includes:\n * - Request interceptor: injects Bearer token from getAccessToken()\n * - Response interceptor (optional): 401 refresh + retry when refreshAccessToken is provided\n */\nexport function createAxiosInstance(config: ApiClientConfig): AxiosInstance {\n  const instance = axios.create({\n    baseURL: config.baseUrl,\n    withCredentials: config.withCredentials ?? false,\n    headers: config.defaultHeaders,\n  });\n\n  // Request interceptor: inject auth token\n  instance.interceptors.request.use(async (reqConfig) => {\n    const token = await config.getAccessToken();\n    if (token) {\n      reqConfig.headers.set('Authorization', `Bearer ${token}`);\n    }\n    return reqConfig;\n  });\n\n  // Response interceptor: 401 refresh + retry (optional)\n  if (config.refreshAccessToken) {\n    const refreshFn = config.refreshAccessToken;\n    const onFailure = config.onRefreshFailure;\n    let refreshPromise: Promise<string> | null = null;\n\n    instance.interceptors.response.use(\n      (response) => response,\n      async (error: AxiosError) => {\n        const originalRequest = error.config as\n          | (AxiosRequestConfig & { _retry?: boolean })\n          | undefined;\n        if (!originalRequest) throw error;\n\n        // Don't retry refresh endpoint to avoid infinite loop\n        const isRefreshRequest = originalRequest.url?.includes('/auth/refresh');\n\n        if (error.response?.status === 401 && !originalRequest._retry && !isRefreshRequest) {\n          originalRequest._retry = true;\n\n          if (!refreshPromise) {\n            refreshPromise = refreshFn()\n              .catch((refreshError) => {\n                onFailure?.();\n                throw refreshError;\n              })\n              .finally(() => {\n                refreshPromise = null;\n              });\n          }\n\n          // All concurrent 401 handlers await the same promise\n          const token = await refreshPromise;\n          originalRequest.headers = originalRequest.headers ?? {};\n          originalRequest.headers['Authorization'] = `Bearer ${token}`;\n          return instance.request(originalRequest);\n        }\n\n        throw error;\n      }\n    );\n  }\n\n  return instance;\n}\n\n/**\n * Singleton configuration for orval-generated code.\n * The consumer must call setApiClientConfig() before using generated functions\n * without passing _axiosInstance explicitly.\n */\nlet _config: ApiClientConfig | null = null;\nlet _instance: AxiosInstance | null = null;\n\n/**\n * Configure the module-level singleton used by orval-generated functions.\n *\n * @param config - API client configuration\n * @param instance - Optional pre-built axios instance. When provided,\n *   getCachedInstance() returns it directly instead of creating a new one.\n *   Use this to share a single instance between the singleton path and\n *   instance-scoped consumers (e.g., CipherBoxClient).\n */\nexport function setApiClientConfig(config: ApiClientConfig, instance?: AxiosInstance): void {\n  _config = config;\n  _instance = instance ?? null;\n}\n\nexport function getApiClientConfig(): ApiClientConfig {\n  if (!_config) throw new Error('API client not configured. Call setApiClientConfig() first.');\n  return _config;\n}\n\nfunction getCachedInstance(): AxiosInstance {\n  if (!_instance) {\n    _instance = createAxiosInstance(getApiClientConfig());\n  }\n  return _instance;\n}\n\nexport const customInstance = async <T>(\n  config: AxiosRequestConfig,\n  options?: AxiosRequestConfig & { _axiosInstance?: AxiosInstance }\n): Promise<T> => {\n  const { _axiosInstance, ...restOptions } = options ?? {};\n\n  if (_axiosInstance) {\n    // Instance-scoped path: the provided instance has its own interceptors for auth\n    const response = await _axiosInstance.request<T>({\n      ...config,\n      ...restOptions,\n      headers: {\n        ...config.headers,\n        ...restOptions.headers,\n      },\n    });\n    return response.data;\n  }\n\n  // Singleton path: uses getCachedInstance() which has its own request interceptor\n  const instance = getCachedInstance();\n\n  const response = await instance.request<T>({\n    ...config,\n    ...restOptions,\n    headers: {\n      ...config.headers,\n      ...restOptions.headers,\n    },\n  });\n\n  return response.data;\n};\n\nexport default customInstance;\n\nexport type ErrorType<Error> = AxiosError<Error>;\nexport type BodyType<BodyData> = BodyData;\n\n// Re-export axios types that consumers need for IPFS upload (progress, cancel)\nexport type { AxiosInstance, AxiosProgressEvent, CancelToken };\nexport { default as axios } from 'axios';\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/lib/api-config.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * API client configuration — single shared axios instance.\n *\n * Creates one axios instance with auth interceptors (token injection + 401 refresh)\n * and registers it as both the orval singleton and the exported `apiAxios` instance.\n *\n * The same instance is used by:\n * - Orval-generated functions (via singleton path in customInstance)\n * - CipherBoxClient (via CipherBoxClientConfig.axiosInstance)\n * - sdk-core IPFS operations (via SdkContext.axiosInstance)\n *\n * This eliminates the dual-path architecture where the singleton and SDK client\n * had separate axios instances with potentially divergent configuration.\n */\nimport {\n  createAxiosInstance,\n  setApiClientConfig,\n  authControllerRefresh,\n} from '@cipherbox/api-client';\nimport { useAuthStore } from '../stores/auth.store';\n\nconst apiUrl =\n  import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL ||\n  (typeof window !== 'undefined' ? `${window.location.origin}/api` : '/api');\n\nconst apiConfig = {\n  baseUrl: apiUrl,\n  getAccessToken: async () => useAuthStore.getState().accessToken || '',\n  withCredentials: true,\n  refreshAccessToken: async () => {\n    // Uses the same apiAxios instance (captured via closure).\n    // Safe because apiAxios is assigned synchronously before any 401 can occur.\n    const response = await authControllerRefresh({}, { _axiosInstance: apiAxios });\n    const newToken = response.accessToken;\n    useAuthStore.getState().setAccessToken(newToken);\n    return newToken;\n  },\n  onRefreshFailure: () => {\n    // Dynamic import to avoid circular dependency at module load time\n    import('../lib/clear-user-stores')\n      .then(({ clearAllUserStores }) => {\n        clearAllUserStores();\n      })\n      .catch(() => {\n        // Chunk load failed — force reload to clear stale session state\n        window.location.reload();\n      });\n  },\n};\n\n/** Shared axios instance used by the entire web app. */\nexport const apiAxios = createAxiosInstance(apiConfig);\n\n// Register as the orval singleton so generated functions work without\n// explicit _axiosInstance. The same instance is reused (no duplicate creation).\nsetApiClientConfig(apiConfig, apiAxios);\n\nexport { apiUrl };\n"
}

> AGENT

Done. Here's the architecture now:

**Before (dual path):**
```
Web app services → generated functions → customInstance → singleton axios instance
CipherBoxClient  → sdk-core           → generated functions → _axiosInstance (separate instance)
```

**After (single path):**
```
api-config.ts creates ONE axios instance (with auth + 401 refresh)
    ↓                          ↓
registered as singleton     passed to CipherBoxClient
    ↓                          ↓
Web app services →         SDK operations →
  generated functions        sdk-core → generated functions
    ↓                          ↓
    └──── same axios instance ─┘
```

One instance, one set of interceptors, one auth token lifecycle. No divergence possible.

     4→area: api

    10→  - apps/api/src/auth/auth.controller.ts:63

    14→  - apps/api/src/auth/controllers/identity.controller.ts:142
    15→  - apps/api/src/auth/controllers/identity.controller.ts:195
    16→  - apps/api/src/auth/controllers/identity.controller.ts:216
    17→  - apps/api/src/device-approval/device-approval.controller.ts:31

    19→
    20→## Problem
    21→
    22→Load tests against local API show high error rates (23/60 uploads failed with 429, 146/174 folder ops failed) despite `BypassableThrottlerGuard` being active and `THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET` configured.
    23→
    24→**Root cause identified:** The API has two layers of rate limiting:
    25→
    26→1. **Controller-level `@UseGuards(ThrottlerGuard)`** — uses `BypassableThrottlerGuard` (bypass works here)
    27→2. **Per-endpoint `@Throttle()` decorators** — override the default limits with stricter per-endpoint caps
    28→
    29→The `@Throttle()` decorator configures limits that the `ThrottlerGuard` enforces. Since `BypassableThrottlerGuard` extends `ThrottlerGuard` and short-circuits in `canActivate()` before the parent checks limits, the bypass **should** work for both layers. However, the load test still shows 429s on IPNS publish — this needs investigation.
    30→
    31→**Possible explanations:**
    32→
    33→1. The `@Throttle` limits are being enforced by a different mechanism (unlikely — NestJS throttler uses the guard for both)
    34→2. The 429s originate from IPFS/Kubo itself, not the CipherBox API throttler (the IPNS publish makes external HTTP calls to the delegated routing service)
    35→3. The bypass header isn't reaching the IPNS publish requests — the SDK's `createAndPublishIpnsRecord` calls through the api-client axios singleton which should have `defaultHeaders`, but this needs verification
    36→4. The per-endpoint `@Throttle` decorator overrides the guard class entirely (unlikely but check NestJS throttler internals)
    37→
    38→**IPNS publish endpoint limits (the bottleneck):**
    39→
    40→- `POST /ipns/publish` — 10 per minute per user
    41→- `POST /ipns/publish-batch` — 5 per minute per user
    42→- `GET /ipns/resolve` — 30 per minute per user
    43→
    44→Each SDK `uploadFile` triggers: 1 IPFS add + 1 IPNS publish (file metadata) + 1 IPNS publish (folder metadata) = 2 IPNS publishes per upload. With 3 concurrent clients doing 20 uploads each, that's 120 IPNS publishes — far exceeding 10/min even per-user.
    45→
    46→**Other per-endpoint `@Throttle` limits:**
    47→
    48→- `POST /auth/login` — 10 per 60s
    49→- `DELETE /auth/account` — 3 per 60s
    50→- `POST /auth/test-login` — 5 per 15min (blocks rapid account creation in load tests)
    51→- `POST /identity/email/send-otp` — 5 per 15min
    52→- `POST /identity/email/verify-otp` — 5 per 15min
    53→- `GET /identity/wallet/nonce` — 10 per 60s
    54→- `POST /identity/wallet` — 5 per 15min
    55→- `POST /device-approval/request` — 3 per 60s
    56→
    57→## Solution
    58→
    59→### Step 1: Verify bypass header reaches IPNS publish
    60→
    61→Add temporary logging in `BypassableThrottlerGuard.canActivate()` to confirm the header is present on IPNS publish requests. If the header is missing, the issue is in the axios `defaultHeaders` propagation.
    62→
    63→### Step 2: Check if 429 comes from API or upstream
    64→
    65→Inspect the 429 response body — the CipherBox API throttler returns `{"statusCode":429,"message":"ThrottlerException: Too Many Requests"}`. If the response body is different, the 429 comes from IPFS/Kubo or the mock IPNS routing service.
    66→
    67→### Step 3: Fix the bypass gap
    68→
    69→If the bypass works at the guard level but `@Throttle` overrides it, the fix is to make `BypassableThrottlerGuard.handleRequest()` also short-circuit (not just `canActivate()`). Alternatively, `@Throttle` just sets metadata that the guard reads — if `canActivate()` returns `true` before calling `super`, the `@Throttle` metadata is never consulted.
    70→
    71→### Step 4: Consider per-endpoint bypass behavior
    72→
    73→Some `@Throttle` limits are security-critical (OTP send, account delete) and should NOT be bypassed even in tests. Others (IPNS publish) are infrastructure protection and should be bypassable. May need a mechanism to mark specific endpoints as bypass-eligible vs always-throttled.
    74→
```

> AGENT

## Investigate throttle bypass gap for per-endpoint Throttle decorators

**Area:** api
**Created:** 2026-03-22 (today)
**Files:** 12 files across guards and controllers

### Problem
Load tests show high 429 error rates despite throttle bypass being configured. Two layers of rate limiting exist: controller-level `@UseGuards(ThrottlerGuard)` (bypass works) and per-endpoint `@Throttle()` decorators with strict limits (10 IPNS publishes/min). The root cause could be the bypass header not reaching IPNS requests, 429s from upstream IPFS/Kubo, or a NestJS throttler interaction.

### Solution
4-step investigation: verify header reaches IPNS publish, check if 429s originate from API or upstream, fix bypass gap if found, and consider per-endpoint bypass eligibility.

---

     1→import { Injectable, ExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/common';
     2→import { ThrottlerGuard } from '@nestjs/throttler';
     3→import { timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto';
     4→
     5→/**
     6→ * Extends ThrottlerGuard with a bypass header for test environments.
     7→ *
     8→ * When THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set, NODE_ENV is not 'production', and
     9→ * a request includes a matching X-Throttle-Bypass header, rate limiting
    10→ * is skipped. This allows SDK E2E and load tests to run against staging
    11→ * without hitting 429s.
    12→ *
    13→ * Security:
    14→ *   - Production always enforces rate limits (NODE_ENV check)
    15→ *   - If the env var is unset/empty, the header is ignored entirely
    16→ *   - Uses crypto.timingSafeEqual to prevent timing attacks
    17→ */
    18→@Injectable()
    19→export class BypassableThrottlerGuard extends ThrottlerGuard {
    20→  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {
    21→    const secret = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET;
    22→
    23→    if (secret && process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production') {
    24→      const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    25→      const rawHeader = request.headers['x-throttle-bypass'];
    26→      const header = Array.isArray(rawHeader) ? rawHeader[0] : rawHeader;
    27→      if (header && safeEqual(header, secret)) {
    28→        return true;
    29→      }
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    return super.canActivate(context);
    33→  }
    34→}
    35→
    36→/** Constant-time string comparison using Node.js crypto. */
    37→function safeEqual(a: string, b: string): boolean {
    38→  const bufA = Buffer.from(a);
    39→  const bufB = Buffer.from(b);
    40→  if (bufA.length !== bufB.length) return false;
    41→  return timingSafeEqual(bufA, bufB);
    42→}
    43→

> AGENT

The investigation reveals the bypass gap is actually **already fixed** by the work in this session:

1. `BypassableThrottlerGuard.canActivate()` returns `true` before `super.canActivate()` — per-endpoint `@Throttle` is never consulted. **Hypothesis 1 ruled out.**
2. IPFS controller has no throttle guard (`@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)` only). **IPFS not the source.**
3. IPNS operations now go through the instance-scoped axios (with `defaultHeaders` including the bypass header). **Hypothesis 3 fixed by the refactor.**
4. The todo was captured in commit `6080a11bf` *before* the bypass propagation fix in `5058d8ec3`.

However, there's one remaining inconsistency: `addToIpfs` and `unpinFromIpfs` use bare `axios.post()` instead of `ctx.axiosInstance`. While the IPFS controller has no throttle guard, this means these calls miss 401 refresh. Let me fix this for consistency:

> TOOL

```
     1→import axios, { type AxiosProgressEvent, type CancelToken } from 'axios';
     2→import type {
     3→  SdkContext,
     4→  IpfsAddResult,
     5→  ProgressCallback,
     6→  DownloadProgressCallback,
     7→} from '../types';
     8→
     9→/**
    10→ * Upload encrypted data to IPFS via backend relay.
    11→ * Uses axios for upload progress tracking.
    12→ *
    13→ * Replaces: apps/web/src/lib/api/ipfs.ts addToIpfs()
    14→ * Change: Takes SdkContext instead of reading useAuthStore.getState()
    15→ */
    16→export async function addToIpfs(
    17→  ctx: SdkContext,
    18→  encryptedData: Uint8Array,
    19→  onProgress?: ProgressCallback,
    20→  cancelToken?: CancelToken
    21→): Promise<IpfsAddResult> {
    22→  const token = await ctx.getAccessToken();
    23→  const blob = new Blob([encryptedData as BlobPart]);
    24→  const formData = new FormData();
    25→  formData.append('file', blob);
    26→
    27→  const response = await axios.post<IpfsAddResult>(`${ctx.apiUrl}/ipfs/upload`, formData, {
    28→    headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
    29→    onUploadProgress: (event: AxiosProgressEvent) => {
    30→      if (event.total && onProgress) {
    31→        onProgress(Math.round((event.loaded * 100) / event.total));
    32→      }
    33→    },
    34→    cancelToken,
    35→  });
    36→  return response.data;
    37→}
    38→
    39→/**
    40→ * Unpin file from IPFS via backend relay.
    41→ *
    42→ * Replaces: apps/web/src/lib/api/ipfs.ts unpinFromIpfs()
    43→ * Change: Takes SdkContext instead of reading useAuthStore.getState()
    44→ */
    45→export async function unpinFromIpfs(ctx: SdkContext, cid: string): Promise<void> {
    46→  const token = await ctx.getAccessToken();
    47→  await axios.post(
    48→    `${ctx.apiUrl}/ipfs/unpin`,
    49→    { cid },
    50→    { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } }
    51→  );
    52→}
    53→
    54→/**
    55→ * Fetch encrypted data from IPFS via backend relay.
    56→ * Supports download progress tracking.
    57→ *
    58→ * Replaces: apps/web/src/lib/api/ipfs.ts fetchFromIpfs()
    59→ * Change: Takes SdkContext instead of reading useAuthStore.getState()
    60→ */
    61→export async function fetchFromIpfs(
    62→  ctx: SdkContext,
    63→  cid: string,
    64→  onProgress?: DownloadProgressCallback
    65→): Promise<Uint8Array> {
    66→  const token = await ctx.getAccessToken();
    67→  const response = await fetch(`${ctx.apiUrl}/ipfs/${cid}`, {
    68→    headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
    69→  });
    70→  if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`Failed to fetch from IPFS: ${response.status}`);
    71→
    72→  const contentLength = response.headers.get('Content-Length');
    73→  if (!onProgress || !contentLength) {
    74→    return new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer());
    75→  }
    76→
    77→  const total = parseInt(contentLength, 10);
    78→  const reader = response.body?.getReader();
    79→  if (!reader) throw new Error('ReadableStream not supported');
    80→
    81→  const chunks: Uint8Array[] = [];
    82→  let loaded = 0;
    83→  while (true) {
    84→    const { done, value } = await reader.read();
    85→    if (done) break;
    86→    chunks.push(value);
    87→    loaded += value.length;
    88→    onProgress(loaded, total);
    89→  }
    90→
    91→  const result = new Uint8Array(loaded);
    92→  let offset = 0;
    93→  for (const chunk of chunks) {
    94→    result.set(chunk, offset);
    95→    offset += chunk.length;
    96→  }
    97→  return result;
    98→}
    99→
```

  "content": "import axios, { type AxiosProgressEvent, type CancelToken } from 'axios';\nimport type {\n  SdkContext,\n  IpfsAddResult,\n  ProgressCallback,\n  DownloadProgressCallback,\n} from '../types';\n\n/**\n * Upload encrypted data to IPFS via backend relay.\n * Uses ctx.axiosInstance when available (gets auth interceptors + default headers),\n * falls back to bare axios with manual token injection.\n */\nexport async function addToIpfs(\n  ctx: SdkContext,\n  encryptedData: Uint8Array,\n  onProgress?: ProgressCallback,\n  cancelToken?: CancelToken\n): Promise<IpfsAddResult> {\n  const blob = new Blob([encryptedData as BlobPart]);\n  const formData = new FormData();\n  formData.append('file', blob);\n\n  const requestConfig = {\n    onUploadProgress: (event: AxiosProgressEvent) => {\n      if (event.total && onProgress) {\n        onProgress(Math.round((event.loaded * 100) / event.total));\n      }\n    },\n    cancelToken,\n  };\n\n  if (ctx.axiosInstance) {\n    const response = await ctx.axiosInstance.post<IpfsAddResult>(\n      '/ipfs/upload',\n      formData,\n      requestConfig\n    );\n    return response.data;\n  }\n\n  // Fallback: bare axios with manual token (no instance available)\n  const token = await ctx.getAccessToken();\n  const response = await axios.post<IpfsAddResult>(`${ctx.apiUrl}/ipfs/upload`, formData, {\n    headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },\n    ...requestConfig,\n  });\n  return response.data;\n}\n\n/**\n * Unpin file from IPFS via backend relay.\n */\nexport async function unpinFromIpfs(ctx: SdkContext, cid: string): Promise<void> {\n  if (ctx.axiosInstance) {\n    await ctx.axiosInstance.post('/ipfs/unpin', { cid });\n    return;\n  }\n\n  const token = await ctx.getAccessToken();\n  await axios.post(\n    `${ctx.apiUrl}/ipfs/unpin`,\n    { cid },\n    { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } }\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * Fetch encrypted data from IPFS via backend relay.\n * Uses native fetch for streaming download progress support.\n * Auth token is obtained from ctx (axiosInstance interceptor isn't used\n * here because fetch provides ReadableStream for progress tracking).\n */\nexport async function fetchFromIpfs(\n  ctx: SdkContext,\n  cid: string,\n  onProgress?: DownloadProgressCallback\n): Promise<Uint8Array> {\n  const token = await ctx.getAccessToken();\n  const response = await fetch(`${ctx.apiUrl}/ipfs/${cid}`, {\n    headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },\n  });\n  if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`Failed to fetch from IPFS: ${response.status}`);\n\n  const contentLength = response.headers.get('Content-Length');\n  if (!onProgress || !contentLength) {\n    return new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer());\n  }\n\n  const total = parseInt(contentLength, 10);\n  const reader = response.body?.getReader();\n  if (!reader) throw new Error('ReadableStream not supported');\n\n  const chunks: Uint8Array[] = [];\n  let loaded = 0;\n  while (true) {\n    const { done, value } = await reader.read();\n    if (done) break;\n    chunks.push(value);\n    loaded += value.length;\n    onProgress(loaded, total);\n  }\n\n  const result = new Uint8Array(loaded);\n  let offset = 0;\n  for (const chunk of chunks) {\n    result.set(chunk, offset);\n    offset += chunk.length;\n  }\n  return result;\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

**Investigation summary for the throttle bypass todo:**

The gap described in the todo has been resolved across multiple commits in this session:

| Concern | Status | Resolution |
|---------|--------|------------|
| Per-endpoint `@Throttle` bypassed? | **No issue** | `canActivate()` returns `true` before `super` — `@Throttle` metadata never consulted |
| Bypass header reaches IPNS calls? | **Fixed** | Instance-scoped axios threads `defaultHeaders` through all IPNS operations |
| Bypass header reaches IPFS calls? | **Fixed** | `addToIpfs`/`unpinFromIpfs` now use `ctx.axiosInstance` (IPFS controller has no throttle, but consistency + 401 refresh) |
| 429s from upstream? | **Likely cause** | Original load test failures were before bypass was propagated (commit `5058d8ec3`) |

All API call paths in sdk-core now flow through the shared axios instance. No more bare `axios.post()` with manual token injection (except `fetchFromIpfs` which uses native `fetch` for streaming).

--- Thread 1 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM51_ggG) ---
  Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
  File: tests/load/src/harness/client-pool.ts:None
  DB ID: 2971978250
  Body (first 400 chars): Load test pool configures the `@cipherbox/api-client` axios singleton once using only clients[0].accessToken. But sdk-core uses orval-generated functions that always call the singleton (e.g., IPNS publish/resolve), so operations from other clients will still authenticate as client[0] on those code paths. This makes multi-client load tests incorrect (and can cause 401/403s or cross-account writes).

--- Thread 2 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM51_ggK) ---
  Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
  File: tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/error-cases.test.ts:None
  DB ID: 2971978254
  Body (first 400 chars): This `setApiClientConfig()` call resets the api-client singleton without `defaultHeaders`. If THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set, this drops the `X-Throttle-Bypass` header and can reintroduce 429s in this suite (the comment says it’s just re-setting the singleton). Consider passing the same `defaultHeaders` as the test harness (or avoid reconfiguring the singleton here by making the harness/fixture res
```

</details>

**Remove `rootIpnsName`, `rootFolderKey`, and `teeKeys` from `CipherBoxClientConfig`; refactor their injection.**

The type definition in `packages/sdk/src/types.ts` incorrectly includes `rootIpnsName`, `rootFolderKey`, and `teeKeys` as constructor config. Per design intent, these are internal state discovered dynamically (root IPNS name via API, root folder key from vault blob, TEE keys from service). The test harness amplifies this: it passes them to the constructor then immediately calls `registerFolder()` with the same values.

The fix requires:
1. Remove `rootIpnsName`, `rootFolderKey`, and `teeKeys` from `CipherBoxClientConfig`
2. Remove the copy logic at `client.ts:55` (`this.internalRootFolderKey = new Uint8Array(config.rootFolderKey)`)
3. Refactor usages at `client.ts:318`, `598`, `939` that reference `this.config.teeKeys` — these should fetch/receive teeKeys through a separate internal mechanism or injectable test helper
4. Update test harness to remove these fields from the constructor call

because these are discovered at runtime; remove those fields from the
CipherBoxClientConfig type, delete the constructor copy that sets
this.internalRootFolderKey from config (the assignment where
this.internalRootFolderKey = new Uint8Array(config.rootFolderKey)), and update
all code paths that read this.config.teeKeys (calls inside CipherBoxClient
methods such as where teeKeys are referenced) to obtain teeKeys via an internal

config; finally, update the test harness to stop passing

the client discover them or call registerFolder(...) only where appropriate.

     1→/**
     2→ * File Upload/Download Workload
     3→ *
     4→ * Parameterized workload that uploads and downloads files of varying sizes.
     5→ * Individual operation failures are recorded as errors but don't abort the workload.
     6→ */
     7→
     8→import type { PoolClient } from '../harness/client-pool';
     9→
    10→export interface FileWorkloadOptions {
    11→  /** Number of files to upload */
    12→  fileCount: number;
    13→  /** Min file size in bytes */
    14→  minSize: number;
    15→  /** Max file size in bytes */
    16→  maxSize: number;
    17→  /** Whether to verify downloads after upload */
    18→  verifyDownloads: boolean;
    19→}
    20→
    21→/**
    22→ * Run a file upload workload on a single client.
    23→ */
    24→export async function runFileWorkload(pc: PoolClient, opts: FileWorkloadOptions): Promise<void> {
    25→  const { fileCount, minSize, maxSize, verifyDownloads } = opts;
    26→  const { client, rootIpnsName, rootFolderKey, metrics } = pc;
    27→
    28→  for (let i = 0; i < fileCount; i++) {
    29→    const size = minSize + Math.floor(Math.random() * (maxSize - minSize));
    30→    const data = new Uint8Array(size);
    31→    // crypto.getRandomValues() has a 65536 byte limit per call
    32→    for (let offset = 0; offset < size; offset += 65536) {
    33→      const chunk = new Uint8Array(data.buffer, offset, Math.min(65536, size - offset));
    34→      crypto.getRandomValues(chunk);
    35→    }
    36→    const fileName = `load-${pc.id}-file-${i}-${size}b.bin`;
    37→
    38→    try {
    39→      // Upload
    40→      await metrics.measure(
    41→        'uploadFile',
    42→        () => client.uploadFile(rootIpnsName, data, fileName, 'application/octet-stream'),
    43→        size
    44→      );
    45→
    46→      // Optionally download and verify
    47→      if (verifyDownloads) {
    48→        const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(rootIpnsName);
    49→        const child = folder?.children.find((c) => c.name === fileName);
    50→        const fileIpnsName =
    51→          child && 'fileMetaIpnsName' in child ? child.fileMetaIpnsName : undefined;
    52→        await metrics.measure(
    53→          'downloadFile',
    54→          async () => {
    55→            if (!fileIpnsName) {
    56→              throw new Error(
    57→                `[Client ${pc.id}] File "${fileName}" not found in folder state after upload`
    58→              );
    59→            }
    60→            const downloaded = await client.downloadFromIpns(fileIpnsName, rootFolderKey);
    61→            if (downloaded.length !== data.length) {
    62→              throw new Error(
    63→                `[Client ${pc.id}] Size mismatch: uploaded ${data.length}, downloaded ${downloaded.length}`
    64→              );
    65→            }
    66→            return downloaded;
    67→          },
    68→          size
    69→        );
    70→      }
    71→    } catch (err) {
    72→      console.warn(
    73→        `[Client ${pc.id}] File upload ${fileName} failed: ${(err as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
    74→      );
    75→    }
    76→  }
    77→}
    78→

    99→      encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey: bytesToHex(encrypted.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey),
   100→      rootIpnsName,
   101→    }),
   102→  });
   103→  if (!initRes.ok) {
   104→    throw new Error(`vault/init failed (${initRes.status}): ${await initRes.text()}`);
   105→  }
   106→
   107→  // 4. Create CipherBoxClient with instance-scoped axios (no singleton needed)
   108→  const client = new CipherBoxClient({
   109→    apiUrl,
   110→    getAccessToken: async () => accessToken,
   111→    vaultKeypair: { publicKey, privateKey },
   112→    rootIpnsName,
   113→    rootFolderKey: vault.rootFolderKey,
   114→    defaultHeaders: axiosDefaultHeaders(),
   115→  });
   116→  client.registerFolder(rootIpnsName, vault.rootFolderKey, vault.rootIpnsKeypair, [], 0n);
   117→

   122→    privateKey,
   123→    rootIpnsName,
   124→    rootFolderKey: vault.rootFolderKey,
   125→    rootIpnsKeypair: vault.rootIpnsKeypair,
   126→    email,
   127→  };
   128→}

   135→  return { ...account, cleanup: () => account.client.destroy() };

5. **mixed-workload.ts weight validation** — Valid, add guard
6. **test-harness.ts rollback** — Valid, add try/catch with cleanup
7. **test-harness.ts remove config fields** — Not applicable. These fields are intentional — `rootIpnsName`, `rootFolderKey`, and `teeKeys` are known at client creation time (provided by the consumer after login/vault load). The SDK doesn't "discover" them.

}

boolean;\n+}\n+\n+/**\n+ * Run a file upload workload on a single client.\n+ */\n+export async function runFileWorkload(pc: PoolClient, opts: FileWorkloadOptions): Promise<void> {\n+  const { fileCount, minSize, maxSize, verifyDownloads } = opts;\n+  const { client, rootIpnsName, rootFolderKey, metrics } = pc;\n+\n+  for (let i = 0; i < fileCount; i++) {\n+    const size = minSize + Math.floor(Math.random() * (maxSize - minSize));\n+    const data = new Uint8Array(size);\n+    // crypto.getRandomValues() has a 65536 byte limit per call\n+    for (let offset = 0; offset < size; offset += 65536) {\n+      const chunk = new Uint8Array(data.buffer, offset, Math.min(65536, size - offset));\n+      crypto.getRandomValues(chunk);\n+    }\n+    const fileName = `load-${pc.id}-file-${i}-${size}b.bin`;\n+\n+    try {\n+      // Upload\n+      await metrics.measure(\n+        'uploadFile',\n+        () => client.uploadFile(rootIpnsName, data, fileName, 'application/octet-stream'),\n+        size\n+      );\n+\n+      // Optionally download and verify\n+      if (verifyDownloads) {\n+        const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(rootIpnsName);\n+        const child = folder?.children.find((c) => c.name === fileName);\n+        const fileIpnsName =\n+          child && 'fileMetaIpnsName' in child ? child.fileMetaIpnsName : undefined;\n+        await metrics.measure(\n+          'downloadFile',\n+          async () => {\n+            if (!fileIpnsName) {\n+              throw new Error(\n+                `[Client ${pc.id}] File \"${fileName}\" not found in folder state after upload`\n+              );\n+            }\n+            const downloaded = await client.downloadFromIpns(fileIpnsName, rootFolderKey);\n+            if (downloaded.length !== data.length) {\n+              throw new Error(\n+                `[Client ${pc.id}] Size mismatch: uploaded ${data.length}, downloaded ${downloaded.length}`\n+              );\n+            }","path":"tests/load/src/workloads/file-workload.ts","commit_id":"b3612f106df7ff592839a411e08bbb539b3cc25c","original_commit_id":"04cf8e4782f2210fd36405878445681cea431231","user":{"login":"FSM1","id":12774278,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjEyNzc0Mjc4","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/12774278?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Fixed function runMixedWorkload(pc: PoolClient, opts: MixedWorkloadOptions): Promise<void> {\n+  const { client, rootIpnsName, metrics } = pc;\n+  const weights = { ...DEFAULT_WEIGHTS, ...opts.weights };\n+ The consumer (web app or test harness) loads the vault, derives the root IPNS name, decrypts the root folder key, and passes them to the constructor. The SDK does not have the private key or vault access needed to discover these independently. This is by design per the zero-knowledge architecture.'",

'http://localhost:3000';\n+const SECRET = process.env.SDK_E2E_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';\n+const THROTTLE_BYPASS = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET ?? '';\n+\n+/**\n+ * Build default headers for fetch calls.\n+ * Includes the throttle bypass header when THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set.\n+ */\n+function fetchHeaders(extra: Record<string, string> = {}): Record<string, string> {\n+  const headers: Record<string, string> = { ...extra };\n+  if (THROTTLE_BYPASS) {\n+    headers['X-Throttle-Bypass'] = THROTTLE_BYPASS;\n+  }\n+  return headers;\n+}\n+\n+/** Default headers for the api-client axios instance (throttle bypass). */\n+function axiosDefaultHeaders(): Record<string, string> | undefined {\n+  return THROTTLE_BYPASS ? { 'X-Throttle-Bypass': THROTTLE_BYPASS } : undefined;\n+}\n+\n+/** Core account data returned by createTestAccount (shared between sdk-e2e and load tests). */\n+export interface TestAccount {\n+  client: CipherBoxClient;\n+  accessToken: string;\n+  publicKey: Uint8Array;\n+  privateKey: Uint8Array;\n+  rootIpnsName: string;\n+  rootFolderKey: Uint8Array;\n+  rootIpnsKeypair: { publicKey: Uint8Array; privateKey: Uint8Array };\n+  email: string;\n+}\n+\n+export interface TestContext extends TestAccount {\n+  /** Destroy the client and zero key material */\n+  cleanup: () => void;\n+}\n+\n+export interface CreateAccountOptions {\n+  apiUrl?: string;\n+  secret?: string;\n+  label: string;\n+  emailPrefix?: string;\n+}\n+\n+/**\n+ * Create a test account with an initialized vault and CipherBoxClient.\n+ * This is the shared core used by both sdk-e2e (createTestContext) and\n+ * load tests (createPoolClient).\n+ */\n+export async function createTestAccount(opts: CreateAccountOptions): Promise<TestAccount> {\n+  const apiUrl = opts.apiUrl ?? API_URL;\n+  const secret = opts.secret ?? SECRET;\n+  const prefix = opts.emailPrefix ?? 'sdk-e2e';\n+  const email = `${prefix}-${opts.label}-${Date.now()}@example.com`;\n+\n+  // 1. 'http://localhost:3000';\n+const SECRET = process.env.SDK_E2E_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';\n+const THROTTLE_BYPASS = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET ?? '';\n+\n+/**\n+ * Build default headers for fetch calls.\n+ * Includes the throttle bypass header when THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set.\n+ */\n+function fetchHeaders(extra: Record<string, string> = {}): Record<string, string> {\n+  const headers: Record<string, string> = { ...extra };\n+  if (THROTTLE_BYPASS) {\n+    headers['X-Throttle-Bypass'] = THROTTLE_BYPASS;\n+  }\n+  return headers;\n+}\n+\n+/** Default headers for the api-client axios instance (throttle bypass). */\n+function axiosDefaultHeaders(): Record<string, string> | undefined {\n+  return THROTTLE_BYPASS ? { 'X-Throttle-Bypass': THROTTLE_BYPASS } : undefined;\n+}\n+\n+/** Core account data returned by createTestAccount (shared between sdk-e2e and load tests). */\n+export interface TestAccount {\n+  client: CipherBoxClient;\n+  accessToken: string;\n+  publicKey: Uint8Array;\n+  privateKey: Uint8Array;\n+  rootIpnsName: string;\n+  rootFolderKey: Uint8Array;\n+  rootIpnsKeypair: { publicKey: Uint8Array; privateKey: Uint8Array };\n+  email: string;\n+}\n+\n+export interface TestContext extends TestAccount {\n+  /** Destroy the client and zero key material */\n+  cleanup: () => void;\n+}\n+\n+export interface CreateAccountOptions {\n+  apiUrl?: string;\n+  secret?: string;\n+  label: string;\n+  emailPrefix?: string;\n+}\n+\n+/**\n+ * Create a test account with an initialized vault and CipherBoxClient.\n+ * This is the shared core used by both sdk-e2e (createTestContext) and\n+ * load tests (createPoolClient).\n+ */\n+export async function createTestAccount(opts: CreateAccountOptions): Promise<TestAccount> {\n+  const apiUrl = opts.apiUrl ?? API_URL;\n+  const secret = opts.secret ?? SECRET;\n+  const prefix = opts.emailPrefix ?? 'sdk-e2e';\n+  const email = `${prefix}-${opts.label}-${Date.now()}@example.com`;\n+\n+  // 1. Authenticate\n+  const loginRes = await fetch(`${apiUrl}/auth/test-login`, {\n+    method: 'POST',\n+    headers: fetchHeaders({ 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }),\n+    body: JSON.stringify({ email, secret }),\n+  });\n+  if (!loginRes.ok) {\n+    throw new Error(`test-login failed (${loginRes.status}): ${await loginRes.text()}`);\n+  }\n+  const { accessToken, publicKeyHex, privateKeyHex } = await loginRes.json();\n+  const publicKey = hexToBytes(publicKeyHex);\n+  const privateKey = hexToBytes(privateKeyHex);\n+\n+  // 2. Initialize vault\n+  const vault = await initializeVault(privateKey);\n+  const encrypted = await encryptVaultKeys(vault, publicKey);\n+  const rootIpnsName = await deriveIpnsName(vault.rootIpnsKeypair.publicKey);\n+\n+  // 3. Register vault on server\n+  const initRes = await fetch(`${apiUrl}/vault/init`, {\n+    method: 'POST',\n+    headers: fetchHeaders({\n+      Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}`,\n+      'Content-Type': 'application/json',\n+    }),\n+    body: JSON.stringify({\n+      ownerPublicKey: bytesToHex(publicKey),\n+      encryptedRootFolderKey: bytesToHex(encrypted.encryptedRootFolderKey),\n+      encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey: bytesToHex(encrypted.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey),\n+      rootIpnsName,\n+    }),\n+  });\n+  if (!initRes.ok) {\n+    throw new Error(`vault/init failed (${initRes.status}): ${await initRes.text()}`);\n+  }\n+\n+  // 4. Create CipherBoxClient with instance-scoped axios (no singleton needed)\n+  const client = new CipherBoxClient({\n+    apiUrl,\n+    getAccessToken: async () => accessToken,\n+    vaultKeypair: { publicKey, privateKey },\n+    rootIpnsName,\n+    rootFolderKey: vault.rootFolderKey,\n+    defaultHeaders: axiosDefaultHeaders(),\n+  });\n+  client.registerFolder(rootIpnsName, vault.rootFolderKey, vault.rootIpnsKeypair, [], 0n);","path":"tests/sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness.ts","commit_id":"04cf8e4782f2210fd36405878445681cea431231","original_commit_id":"04cf8e4782f2210fd36405878445681cea431231","user":{"login":"FSM1","id":12774278,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjEyNzc0Mjc4","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/12774278?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Not applicable. `rootIpnsName`, `rootFolderKey`, and `teeKeys` are intentional config fields — they are known at client creation time (provided by the consumer after login/vault load), not discovered dynamically by the SDK. The consumer (web app or test harness) loads the vault, derives the root IPNS name, decrypts the root folder key, and passes them to the constructor. The SDK does not have the private key or vault access needed to discover these independently. This is by design per the zero-knowledge architecture.","created_at":"2026-03-22T21:27:53Z","updated_at":"2026-03-22T21:27:53Z","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/318#discussion_r2972117596","pull_request_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/318","_links":{"self":{"href":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/comments/2972117596"},"html":{"href":"https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/318#discussion_r2972117596"},"pull_request":{"href":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/318"}},"reactions":{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/comments/2972117596/reactions","total_count":0,"+1":0,"-1":0,"laugh":0,"hooray":0,"confused":0,"heart":0,"rocket":0,"eyes":0},"start_line":null,"original_start_line":107,"start_side":"RIGHT","line":null,"original_line":116,"side":"RIGHT","in_reply_to_id":2972104519,"author_association":"OWNER","original_position":116,"position":1,"subject_type":"line"}

**Wipe the harness-owned secrets during teardown.**

Line 86 only clears the SDK’s internal copies. `PoolClient` still retains the caller-owned `privateKey`, `rootFolderKey`, and `rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey`, so the pool keeps raw key material alive after cleanup.

    80→/**
    81→ * Destroy all clients in the pool and delete test accounts.
    82→ */
    83→export async function destroyClientPool(pool: PoolClient[]): Promise<void> {
    84→  console.log(`Cleaning up ${pool.length} test accounts...`);
    85→  for (const pc of pool) {
    86→    pc.client.destroy();
    87→    await deleteTestAccount(pc, API_URL);
    88→  }
    89→}
    90→
    91→/**
    92→ * Aggregate metrics from all pool clients, print summary, and return metrics.
    93→ * Extracts the repeated boilerplate from every load test scenario.
    94→ */
    95→export async function aggregateAndReport(
    96→  scenarioName: string,
    97→  pool: PoolClient[]
    98→): Promise<OperationMetrics[]> {
    99→  const allMetrics = new MetricsCollector();
   100→
   101→  let minTimestamp = Infinity;
   102→  let maxTimestamp = 0;
   103→
   104→  for (const pc of pool) {
   105→    for (const sample of pc.metrics.getReadonlySamples()) {
   106→      allMetrics.record(sample);
   107→      if (sample.timestamp < minTimestamp) minTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
   108→      if (sample.timestamp > maxTimestamp) maxTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
   109→    }
   110→  }
   111→
   112→  // Set collector timing from sample timestamps for accurate throughput calculation
   113→  allMetrics.setElapsedMs(maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 1);
   114→
   115→  const totalDuration = maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 0;
   116→  const metrics = allMetrics.getMetrics();
   117→
   118→  printSummary(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
   119→
   120→  // Write JSON report to file for CI artifact upload
   121→  const reportJson = toJsonReport(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
   122→  console.log('\nJSON Report:\n' + reportJson);
   123→
   124→  try {
   125→    const fs = await import('node:fs/promises');
   126→    const slug = scenarioName.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-');
   127→    await fs.writeFile(`metrics-${slug}.json`, reportJson);
   128→  } catch {
   129→    // Non-fatal — CI artifact upload will just find no files
   130→  }
   131→
   132→  return metrics;
   133→}
   134→

-0,0 +1,133 @@\n+/**\n+ * Load Test Client Pool\n+ *\n+ * Manages N CipherBoxClient instances, each with its own test account.\n+ * Creates accounts in parallel, distributes workloads, and collects metrics.\n+ *\n+ * Reuses the shared createTestAccount/deleteTestAccount from sdk-e2e\n+ * to avoid duplicating the 5-step account provisioning sequence.\n+ */\n+\n+import {\n+  createTestAccount,\n+  deleteTestAccount,\n+  type TestAccount,\n+} from '../../../sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness';\n+import { MetricsCollector, type OperationMetrics } from './metrics';\n+import { printSummary, toJsonReport } from './reporter';\n+\n+const API_URL = process.env.LOAD_TEST_API_URL ?? 'http://localhost:3000';\n+const SECRET = process.env.LOAD_TEST_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';\n+\n+export interface PoolClient extends TestAccount {\n+  id: number;\n+  metrics: MetricsCollector;\n+}\n+\n+export interface ClientPoolOptions {\n+  clientCount: number;\n+  label: string;\n+}\n+\n+/**\n+ * Create a pool of N authenticated CipherBoxClient instances.\n+ *\n+ * Each client has its own test account, vault, metrics collector, and\n+ * instance-scoped axios (no shared singleton).\n+ */\n+export async function createClientPool(opts: ClientPoolOptions): Promise<PoolClient[]> {\n+  const { clientCount, label } = opts;\n+  console.log(`Creating ${clientCount} test accounts for \"${label}\"...`);\n+  const start = performance.now();\n+\n+  // Create accounts in parallel batches of 5 to avoid overwhelming the API\n+  const clients: PoolClient[] = [];\n+  const batchSize = 5;\n+\n+  for (let batch = 0; batch < clientCount; batch += batchSize) {\n+    const batchEnd = Math.min(batch + batchSize, clientCount);\n+    const promises = [];\n+\n+    for (let i = batch; i < batchEnd; i++) {\n+      promises.push(\n+        createTestAccount({\n+          apiUrl: API_URL,\n+          secret: SECRET,\n+          label: `${label}-${i}`,\n+          emailPrefix: 'load',\n+        }).then((account): PoolClient => ({ ...account, id: i, metrics: new MetricsCollector() }))\n+      );\n+    }\n+\n+    const results = await Promise.allSettled(promises);\n+    for (const result of results) {\n+      if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {\n+        clients.push(result.value);\n+      } else {\n+        console.warn(`Failed to create pool client: ${result.reason}`);\n+      }\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  const elapsed = performance.now() - start;\n+  console.log(\n+    `Created ${clients.length}/${clientCount} clients in ${(elapsed / 1000).toFixed(1)}s`\n+  );\n+\n+  return clients;\n+}\n+\n+/**\n+ * Destroy all clients in the pool and delete test accounts.\n+ */\n+export async function destroyClientPool(pool: PoolClient[]): Promise<void> {\n+  console.log(`Cleaning up ${pool.length} test accounts...`);\n+  for (const pc of pool) {\n+    pc.client.destroy();\n+    await deleteTestAccount(pc, API_URL);\n+  }\n+}\n+\n+/**\n+ * Aggregate metrics from all pool clients, print summary, and return metrics.\n+ * Extracts the repeated boilerplate from every load test scenario.\n+ */\n+export async function aggregateAndReport(\n+  scenarioName: string,\n+  pool: PoolClient[]\n+): Promise<OperationMetrics[]> {\n+  const allMetrics = new MetricsCollector();\n+\n+  let minTimestamp = Infinity;\n+  let maxTimestamp = 0;\n+\n+  for (const pc of pool) {\n+    for (const sample of pc.metrics.getReadonlySamples()) {\n+      allMetrics.record(sample);\n+      if (sample.timestamp < minTimestamp) minTimestamp = sample.timestamp;\n+      if (sample.timestamp > maxTimestamp) maxTimestamp = sample.timestamp;\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  // Set collector timing from sample timestamps for accurate throughput calculation\n+  allMetrics.setElapsedMs(maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 1);\n+\n+  const totalDuration = maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 0;\n+  const metrics = allMetrics.getMetrics();\n+\n+  printSummary(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);\n+\n+  // Write JSON report to file for CI artifact upload\n+  const reportJson = toJsonReport(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);\n+  console.log('\\nJSON Report:\\n' + reportJson);\n+\n+  try {\n+    const fs = await import('node:fs/promises');\n+    const slug = scenarioName.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-');\n+    await fs.writeFile(`metrics-${slug}.json`, reportJson);\n+  } catch {\n+    // Non-fatal — CI artifact upload will just find no files\n+  }","path":"tests/load/src/harness/client-pool.ts","commit_id":"d768edfd698de651e4c74665236ddd159346f959","original_commit_id":"b3612f106df7ff592839a411e08bbb539b3cc25c","user":{"login":"FSM1","id":12774278,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjEyNzc0Mjc4","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/12774278?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Fixed in d768edf.      1→import { Injectable, ExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/common';
     2→import { ThrottlerGuard } from '@nestjs/throttler';
     3→import { timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto';
     4→
     5→/**
     6→ * Extends ThrottlerGuard with a bypass header for test environments.
     7→ *
     8→ * When THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set, NODE_ENV is not 'production', and
     9→ * a request includes a matching X-Throttle-Bypass header, rate limiting
    10→ * is skipped. This allows SDK E2E and load tests to run against staging
    11→ * without hitting 429s.
    12→ *
    13→ * Security:
    14→ *   - Production always enforces rate limits (NODE_ENV check)
    15→ *   - If the env var is unset/empty, the header is ignored entirely
    16→ *   - Uses crypto.timingSafeEqual to prevent timing attacks
    17→ */
    18→@Injectable()
    19→export class BypassableThrottlerGuard extends ThrottlerGuard {
    20→  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {
    21→    const secret = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET;
    22→
    23→    if (secret && process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production') {
    24→      const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    25→      const rawHeader = request.headers['x-throttle-bypass'];
    26→      const header = Array.isArray(rawHeader) ? rawHeader[0] : rawHeader;
    27→      if (header && safeEqual(header, secret)) {
    28→        return true;
    29→      }
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    return super.canActivate(context);
    33→  }
    34→}
    35→
    36→/** Constant-time string comparison using Node.js crypto. */
    37→function safeEqual(a: string, b: string): boolean {
    38→  const bufA = Buffer.from(a);
    39→  const bufB = Buffer.from(b);
    40→  if (bufA.length !== bufB.length) return false;
    41→  return timingSafeEqual(bufA, bufB);
    42→}
    43→

ThrottlerGuard.canActivate so we can track when the parent is called\nconst superCanActivate = jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(true);\njest.spyOn(ThrottlerGuard.prototype, 'canActivate').mockImplementation(superCanActivate);\n\nfunction createMockContext(headers: Record<string, string | string[]> = {}): ExecutionContext {\n  return {\n    switchToHttp: () => ({\n      getRequest: () => ({ headers }),\n      getResponse: () => ({}),\n    }),\n    getHandler: () => ({}),\n    getClass: () => ({}),\n  } as unknown as ExecutionContext;\n}\n\ndescribe('BypassableThrottlerGuard', () => {\n  let guard: BypassableThrottlerGuard;\n  const originalEnv = { ...process.env };\n\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    guard = Object.create(BypassableThrottlerGuard.prototype);\n    superCanActivate.mockClear();\n    process.env = { ...originalEnv };\n  });\n\n  afterAll(() => {\n    process.env = originalEnv;\n  });\n\n  it('should bypass when secret matches and NODE_ENV is not production', async () => {\n    process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET = 'test-secret';\n    process.env.NODE_ENV = 'test';\n\n    const ctx = createMockContext({ 'x-throttle-bypass': 'test-secret' });\n    const result = await guard.canActivate(ctx);\n\n    expect(result).toBe(true);\n    expect(superCanActivate).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  it('should fall through to parent when secret does not match', async () => {\n    process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET = 'test-secret';\n    process.env.NODE_ENV = 'test';\n\n    const ctx = createMockContext({ 'x-throttle-bypass': 'wrong-secret' });\n    await guard.canActivate(ctx);\n\n    expect(superCanActivate).toHaveBeenCalledWith(ctx);\n  });\n\n  it('should fall through to parent when header is missing', async () => {\n    process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET = 'test-secret';\n    process.env.NODE_ENV = 'test';\n\n    const ctx = createMockContext({});\n    await guard.canActivate(ctx);\n\n    expect(superCanActivate).toHaveBeenCalledWith(ctx);\n  });\n\n  it('should fall through to parent in production even with valid header', async () => {\n    process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET = 'test-secret';\n    process.env.NODE_ENV = 'production';\n\n    const ctx = createMockContext({ 'x-throttle-bypass': 'test-secret' });\n    await guard.canActivate(ctx);\n\n    expect(superCanActivate).toHaveBeenCalledWith(ctx);\n  });\n\n  it('should fall through to parent when THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is not set', async () => {\n    delete process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET;\n    process.env.NODE_ENV = 'test';\n\n    const ctx = createMockContext({ 'x-throttle-bypass': 'anything' });\n    await guard.canActivate(ctx);\n\n process.env.NODE_ENV = 'test';\n\n    const ctx = createMockContext({ 'x-throttle-bypass': ['test-secret', 'other'] });\n    const result = await guard.canActivate(ctx);\n\n ## Summary of Renaming Scope

**Critical files that MUST be renamed/updated:**

1. **Directory:** `tests/e2e/` → `tests/ui-e2e/`
2. **Package name in package.json:** `@cipherbox/e2e` → `@cipherbox/ui-e2e`
3. **Root package.json scripts:** `test:e2e` and `test:e2e:headed` references
4. **GitHub workflow files:**
   - `.github/workflows/e2e.yml` (job names, filter references)
   - `.github/workflows/release-gate.yml` (verify-e2e job)
   - `.github/workflows/tag-staging.yml` (web-e2e job)
5. **Release config:** `release-please-config.json` extra-files array
6. **Documentation:** `tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`, `tests/e2e/README.md`, `.learnings/` files, `.claude/` command files
7. **Playwright configs:** Update inline comments referencing the directory

**Safe to ignore:**

- References to `e2e-test-secret-ci-only` or `e2e-test-jwt-secret-key-for-ci` in env vars (these are values, not references)
- References to `e2e-desktop.yml` or `sdk-e2e.yml` (different test suites)
- pnpm-lock.yaml (auto-updates after package.json changes)

5432:5432\n        options: >-\n          --health-cmd pg_isready\n          --health-interval 10s\n          --health-timeout 5s\n          --health-retries 5\n\n      ipfs:\n        image: ipfs/kubo:v0.34.0\n        ports:\n          - 5001:5001\n          - 8080:8080\n        options: >-\n          --health-cmd \"ipfs id\"\n          --health-interval 10s\n          --health-timeout 5s\n          --health-retries 10\n          --health-start-period 30s\n\n      redis:\n        image: redis:7-alpine\n        ports:\n          - 6379:6379\n        options: >-\n          --health-cmd \"redis-cli ping\"\n          --health-interval 10s\n          --health-timeout 5s\n          --health-retries 5\n\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout code\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\n        with:\n          ref: ${{ inputs.ref || github.sha }}\n\n      - name: Setup pnpm\n        uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4\n        with:\n          version: 10\n\n      - name: Setup Node.js\n        uses: actions/setup-node@v4\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n          cache: 'pnpm'\n\n      -     89→  verify-e2e:
    90→    name: Verify Web E2E Passed
    91→    needs: detect-changes

   106→      - name: Wait for Web E2E and verify it passed

   116→          MAX_ATTEMPTS=30

   129→            fi

   133→              exit 1
   134→            fi
   135→
   136→            echo "Attempt ${attempt}/${MAX_ATTEMPTS}: E2E still running, waiting ${POLL_INTERVAL}s..."
   137→            sleep $POLL_INTERVAL
   138→          done
   139→
   140→          if [ "$E2E_CONCLUSION" != "success" ]; then
   141→            echo "::error::E2E Tests did not pass on main (conclusion: ${E2E_CONCLUSION})"
   142→            exit 1
   143→          fi
   144→          echo "Web E2E: passed"
   145→
   146→      - name: Verify Desktop E2E (if desktop changed)
   147→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--') && needs.detect-changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   148→        env:
   149→          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   150→          PREV_TAG: ${{ needs.detect-changes.outputs.prev_tag }}
   151→        run: |
   152→          MAIN_SHA=$(gh api repos/${{ github.repository }}/git/ref/heads/main --jq '.object.sha')
   153→          echo "Desktop changes detected since ${PREV_TAG} — verifying Desktop E2E"
   154→
   155→          # Helper: check if a workflow run actually executed desktop-e2e jobs
   156→          # (not skipped due to change detection). Returns 0 if tests ran, 1 if skipped, 2 on API error.
   157→          run_executed_tests() {
   158→            local run_id=$1
   159→            local executed
   160→            if ! executed=$(gh api "repos/${{ github.repository }}/actions/runs/${run_id}/jobs" \
   161→              --jq '[.jobs[] | select(.name | startswith("Desktop E2E")) | select(.conclusion == "success")] | length'); then
   162→              echo "::error::Failed to query jobs for run ${run_id} (API error or rate limit)"
   163→              return 2
   164→            fi
   165→            [ "${executed:-0}" -gt 0 ]
   166→          }
   167→
   168→          # First check if there's a desktop E2E run for HEAD (may still be running)
   169→          HEAD_RUN=$(gh run list --repo ${{ github.repository }} \
   170→            --workflow=desktop-e2e.yml --branch=main --limit=50 \
   171→            --json headSha,conclusion,status,databaseId \
   172→            --jq "[.[] | select(.headSha == \"${MAIN_SHA}\")][0]")
   173→
   174→          HEAD_STATUS=$(echo "$HEAD_RUN" | jq -r '.status // empty')
   175→
   176→          if [ -n "$HEAD_STATUS" ]; then
   177→            HEAD_RUN_ID=$(echo "$HEAD_RUN" | jq -r '.databaseId')
   178→            echo "Desktop E2E run exists for HEAD (run ${HEAD_RUN_ID}) — polling until complete..."
   179→            MAX_ATTEMPTS=40
   180→            POLL_INTERVAL=30
   181→
   182→            for attempt in $(seq 1 $MAX_ATTEMPTS); do
   183→              CONCLUSION=$(gh run view "$HEAD_RUN_ID" --repo ${{ github.repository }} \
   184→                --json conclusion \
   185→                --jq '.conclusion // empty')
   186→
   187→              if [ -n "$CONCLUSION" ]; then
   188→                if [ "$CONCLUSION" != "success" ]; then
   189→                  echo "::error::Desktop E2E Tests did not pass on main (conclusion: ${CONCLUSION})"
   190→                  exit 1
   191→                fi
   192→                # Verify the desktop-e2e jobs actually ran (not skipped)
   193→                run_executed_tests "$HEAD_RUN_ID" && rc=0 || rc=$?
   194→                if [ "$rc" -eq 2 ]; then
   195→                  exit 1
   196→                elif [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]; then
   197→                  echo "Desktop E2E: passed (at HEAD, tests executed)"
   198→                  exit 0
   199→                else
   200→                  echo "Desktop E2E run at HEAD succeeded but tests were skipped (no desktop changes detected by workflow)."
   201→                  echo "Falling through to search for a run that actually executed tests..."
   202→                  break
   203→                fi
   204→              fi
   205→
   206→              if [ "$attempt" -eq "$MAX_ATTEMPTS" ]; then
   207→                echo "::error::Desktop E2E run did not complete within $(( MAX_ATTEMPTS * POLL_INTERVAL / 60 )) minutes."
   208→                exit 1
   209→              fi
   210→
   211→              echo "Attempt ${attempt}/${MAX_ATTEMPTS}: Desktop E2E still running, waiting ${POLL_INTERVAL}s..."
   212→              sleep $POLL_INTERVAL
   213→            done
   214→          fi
   215→
   216→          # No desktop E2E run for HEAD (or HEAD run skipped tests) — find the
   217→          # most recent run in the release range that actually executed tests
   218→          echo "Searching for a Desktop E2E run that executed tests since ${PREV_TAG}..."
   219→
   220→          if [ -z "$PREV_TAG" ]; then
   221→            echo "::error::No previous release tag and no Desktop E2E run for HEAD. Cannot verify desktop changes."
   222→            exit 1
   223→          fi
   224→
   225→          # Get commit SHAs in the release range
   226→          RANGE_COMMITS=$(gh api "repos/${{ github.repository }}/compare/${PREV_TAG}...${MAIN_SHA}" \
   227→            --jq '[.commits[].sha] | join("\n")' 2>/dev/null || echo "")
   228→
   229→          if [ -z "$RANGE_COMMITS" ]; then
   230→            echo "::error::Could not determine commits in range ${PREV_TAG}..${MAIN_SHA}"
   231→            exit 1
   232→          fi
   233→
   234→          # Get recent completed desktop E2E runs (newest first) and find the
   235→          # most recent one whose commit is within the release range AND whose
   236→          # desktop-e2e jobs actually ran (not skipped)
   237→          RUNS=$(gh run list --repo ${{ github.repository }} \
   238→            --workflow=desktop-e2e.yml --branch=main --limit=50 \
   239→            --json headSha,conclusion,status,databaseId \
   240→            --jq '.[] | select(.status == "completed") | "\(.headSha) \(.conclusion) \(.databaseId)"')
   241→
   242→          LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA=""
   243→          LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION=""
   244→
   245→          while IFS=" " read -r sha conclusion run_id; do
   246→            if [ -z "$sha" ]; then
   247→              continue
   248→            fi
   249→            if echo "$RANGE_COMMITS" | grep -Fq "$sha"; then
   250→              if [ "$conclusion" != "success" ]; then
   251→                # Found a failed run in range — report it immediately
   252→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA="$sha"
   253→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION="$conclusion"
   254→                break
   255→              fi
   256→              # Success run — verify tests actually executed
   257→              run_executed_tests "$run_id" && rc=0 || rc=$?
   258→              if [ "$rc" -eq 2 ]; then
   259→                exit 1
   260→              elif [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]; then
   261→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA="$sha"
   262→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION="$conclusion"
   263→                LATEST_IN_RANGE_RUN_ID="$run_id"
   264→                break
   265→              else
   266→                echo "Skipping run ${run_id} at ${sha} — tests were skipped (no desktop changes detected by workflow)"
   267→              fi
   268→            fi
   269→          done <<EOF
   270→          $RUNS
   271→          EOF
   272→
   273→          if [ -z "$LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA" ]; then
   274→            echo "::error::No Desktop E2E run found (with tests actually executed) for any commit in ${PREV_TAG}..HEAD. Desktop code changed but was never verified."
   275→            exit 1
   276→          fi
   277→
   278→          if [ "$LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION" != "success" ]; then
   279→            echo "::error::Most recent Desktop E2E run in range (at ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA}) did not succeed (conclusion: ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_CONCLUSION})."
   280→            exit 1
   281→          fi
   282→
   283→          echo "Desktop E2E: passed (run ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_RUN_ID} at ${LATEST_IN_RANGE_SHA} with tests executed)"
   284→
   285→      - name: Skip Desktop E2E (no desktop changes)
   286→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--') && needs.detect-changes.outputs.desktop != 'true'
   287→        run: echo "No desktop-related changes since last release — skipping Desktop E2E gate"
   288→
   289→      - name: Summary
   290→        if: startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--')
   291→        run: echo "All E2E gates passed — release is safe to merge."
   292→
```

> AGENT

The issue is the job name rename. I changed:

```yaml
# Before (line 90)
name: Verify E2E Passed

# After  
name: Verify Web E2E Passed
```

If branch protection requires the check `Release Gate / Verify E2E Passed`, the renamed job creates a new check name `Release Gate / Verify Web E2E Passed` — which doesn't match the required check. The old check appears as "expected — waiting for status" forever, blocking merge.

The fix: revert the job display name (branch protection references it), keep everything else renamed.

  "new_string": "    name: Verify E2E Passed"
